## developer

<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Worktrees
- Prefer reusing a suitable active worktree. Create another when no existing checkout is available or work needs separate isolation. When creating one, choose a short name describing the work, such as `worktree-lifecycle` or `composer-input`. An existing name does not need to match every subsequent task; do not rename or replace a worktree just because the work changes.
- Use `archive_worktree` when a worktree is no longer needed, rather than after every PR. Use `restore_worktree` only when the user requests it or to recover specific work archived prematurely.
- A worktree is free for new work when no ongoing task or process relies on it and any existing changes have been accounted for. Prepare the appropriate branch and base before starting new work. Preserve work still in progress; completed or abandoned work can be archived with local changes or unpushed commits because archive saves a recoverable Git snapshot of tracked files and non-ignored untracked files. Ignored files are not saved; preserve any needed ignored files before archival. Do not delete files just to make a worktree eligible for archival. Preserve pinned, shared, or in-use worktrees when cleaning up. Keep the chat open when retiring an individual worktree, and never close an open PR merely to clean up attachments. Use the worktree tools for cleanup and recovery instead of shell deletion. Check at these lifecycle transitions, not every turn.
- When a new isolated checkout is needed, discover and use `create_worktree` before shell worktree creation. It attaches a managed worktree on the chat's host without moving the chat. Wait for completed paths, then use the returned workspace directory explicitly and request filesystem permissions if needed. Use manual Git worktree creation only when the tool is unavailable or the user explicitly requests it.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

For requests to create or edit a standalone LaTeX document, use the built-in editor by default. Create or edit the .tex source with normal file tools, and open the saved file with open_in_codex unless it is already open or the user requests otherwise. Keep follow-up edits in that same file and editor. Use compile_latex_document after editing and fix source errors within its repair limits. Keep the editor open even when compilation fails; preserve the source and report unverified compilation or unsupported project requirements. Discover these tools if deferred. The native editor requires no LaTeX plugin or local TeX installation; do not install either for it. Ordinary math explanations stay in chat.

<multi_agent_role>You are `/root`, the primary agent in a team of agents collaborating to fulfill the user's goals.

At the start of your turn, you are the active agent.
You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents.
All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent without triggering a turn.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.
You can decide how much context you want to propagate to your sub-agents with the `fork_turns` parameter.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
They may be addressed as to=/root

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

Read that folder\'s `CLAUDE.md` before touching anything in it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The primary/mirror boundary (D4 — read before regenerating anything)\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery file in a student folder is one of two kinds, and the distinction decides what a rebuild may overwrite:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Primary — the brain is the only home.** Transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, study plans, anything Joshua or an agent authored about the student. **Never regenerate, never overwrite; retain forever.**\\r\\n- **Mirror — a projection of a live backend.** Session/booking state (Cal.com + Google Calendar), homework/dossier/publish state (LSAT U Supabase), payment events (Stripe), correspondence indexes (Gmail). On any disagreement the backend wins, silently.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Convention:** mirror files MUST be named `*.generated.md` (or carry `generated: true` frontmatter). A file without that marker is primary by definition and must be treated as precious. Pre-2026-07-16 files (`email-context.md`, `payment-links.md`, `transcripts.md`) predate the marker — treat as primary until individually re-derived and renamed. No Markdown file here is ever the source of operational truth — no package balances, no running totals (all services are paid upfront; Cal.com answers sessions-used).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Standard files per student folder\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is the target layout, not a checklist to satisfy with empty files. Create a record when its source or a real teaching judgment supports it. Leave an absent record absent until there is verified content to put in it.\\r\\n\\r\\n- `<student-slug>.md` — internal entry page and folder router (primary). Its `## Open this record` block links only to local files that exist.\\r\\n- `email-context.md` — synthesized Gmail context.\\r\\n- `email-correspondence.md` — Gmail message-id corpus for the student\'s confirmed address.\\r\\n- `payment-links.md` — verified payment, billing, and scheduling links.\\r\\n- `transcripts.md` — transcript and meeting-note inventory. Its `## Local session files` table links directly to every full local Markdown export and every session package, newest first. A Google Doc link is provenance, never the only way to read a session.\\r\\n- `teaching-notes.md` — durable, evidence-backed learning judgments and teaching patterns (primary).\\r\\n- `open-loops.md` — unfinished follow-ups and pending decisions (primary).\\r\\n- `live-status.generated.md` — currently a refreshable LSAT U booking-pass mirror. Calendar, Gmail, and payment state remain in their live systems; this file does not yet summarize them.\\r\\n- `transcript-downloads/` — preserved local exports of Gemini notes (source evidence; do not hand-edit). A raw export here is not itself a completed session package.\\r\\n- `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-session-name/` — new self-contained session packages.\\r\\n- `assignment-log.md` — running log of assigned work and notification evidence so repeats are avoided (primary).\\r\\n- `Practice_Test_Reports/` — the student\'s PT score reports as received (intake form, screenshots, exports). Raw evidence — read for diagnosis, never edit.\\r\\n- `Student_Specific_Homework/` — work assigned to this student specifically. Distinct from the shared banks in `corpus/Curriculum/*/Homework/`, which are the source material it draws from.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Conventions\\r\\n\\r\\n- New student → new folder `students/<slug>/` with a `<slug>.md` profile page.\\r\\n- A consultation followed by a subsequent student session establishes onboarding. Check the Calendar sequence and identity before creating the folder; record any uncertainty about attendance or contact aliases. Consultation-only records stay in `_other-sessions/`.\\r\\n- When Joshua explicitly says a student relationship is over or to close a folder, move the preserved record to `students/non-students/<slug>/`, remove it from the current student index, and refresh the generated roster. Do not delete the record or silently infer closure from inactivity.\\r\\n- New session → `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-session-name/` containing `transcript.md`, `summary.md`, and `topic-map.md`.\\r\\n- Keep the original full Gemini export as a local Markdown file in `transcript-downloads/` when available. Build the session package from verified session evidence, and link both the export and package from `transcripts.md`; older exports may predate the package convention.\\r\\n- `transcript.md` preserves the raw source. `summary.md` records the auto-saved summary, action items, and appended corrections. `topic-map.md` is a timestamped link index to relevant LSAT concepts, student records, and source material.\\r\\n- Session summaries are append-only. Later corrections go in a dated correction section and never erase the original summary.\\r\\n- Generated operational state is clearly marked with `generated: true` and never overrides primary teaching records.\\r\\n- Wikilink student pages into corpus concepts and LSAT U material as they become relevant.\\r\\n- Tier A note: transcripts and correspondence are trackable (Tier-A amendment, 2026-07-16). True secrets (credentials, IDs, payment instruments) still never enter Git — `private/` only.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Internal routing contract after each session\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Match the Calendar event and Gemini Doc by guest email, date, and content. Preserve the complete source export in `transcript-downloads/`; never rely on a Drive link as the record.\\r\\n2. Create or update the session\'s `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-session-name/` package without replacing a primary transcript or summary. Record any source mismatch as a dated correction.\\r\\n3. Add a newest-first row to `transcripts.md` under `## Local session files`: date, direct local full-export link, local package links, and source/status. Include raw-only historical exports and packages even when the other half is absent.\\r\\n4. Update evidence-backed `teaching-notes.md` and `open-loops.md`. Refresh the student\'s `<slug>.md` `## Open this record` block so Joshua, Claude, and Codex can reach the latest work in two clicks. Link only to files that exist.\\r\\n5. Check that all local links resolve, that the full Markdown export contains the transcript rather than only Gemini\'s summary, and that the session index has no unlinked export or package. Leave attendance and missing-source uncertainty explicit.\\r\\n6. Run `python system/scripts/check-student-records.py` from the repo root before calling intake complete. It checks all student routers and every local Markdown export and session package link.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Operating contract\\r\\n\\r\\nFor each new consultation or transcript, Jay 2 must report what was found, what was created or updated, what was inferred, what remains missing, what is recommended, and what Joshua should confirm or reject. During the learning period, no student-facing message, assignment, or platform action is implied by record preparation alone.\\r\\n"}'}]

## assistant

The student records define intake as preserving each full Gemini export, building a session package, and updating the student’s teaching notes and follow-ups. I’m also using the session-recap skill for the student-facing notes it calls for; those will stay as drafts.

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 4.8 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"i":0,"status":"fulfilled","output":"# Post-session note quality reference\\r\\n\\r\\nCanonical source: `students/sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse that note as the quality bar for post-session notes. Preserve its method, not its\\r\\ntopic-specific wording. A strong note:\\r\\n\\r\\n- reads as lecture notes, not an email or a transcript summary;\\r\\n- opens directly with the governing concept;\\r\\n- separates ideas with descriptive headings and generous spacing;\\r\\n- defines the important distinction before asking the student to use it;\\r\\n- turns Joshua\'s explanation into a reusable method;\\r\\n- uses compact tables, numbered steps, and diagrams only when they reduce cognitive load;\\r\\n- includes valid moves, invalid moves, boundary cases, and a way to test the answer;\\r\\n- gives examples that stand alone without requiring memory of a question from days ago;\\r\\n- ends with concrete practice supported by the session; and\\r\\n- keeps private teaching judgments below the `Notes for me (not posted)` divider.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Reference structure\\r\\n\\r\\nThe Sofia note uses this content-shaped progression:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Concept title.** `Conditional Must Be True: Facts, Rules, and Bridges`\\r\\n2. **Direct orientation.** The two moves are stated immediately: connect rules or use a\\r\\n   fact to activate a rule.\\r\\n3. **Method at a glance.** A small table distinguishes chaining from triggering.\\r\\n4. **Foundational distinction.** Facts and rules are defined before either is applied.\\r\\n5. **First method.** Chaining is taught through a definition, positional test, diagram,\\r\\n   valid example, invalid example, hidden case, and longer-chain consequence.\\r\\n6. **Second method.** Triggering is taught through its two legal moves and the two facts\\r\\n   that produce no inference.\\r\\n7. **Decision aid.** A four-row table makes valid and invalid directions visible.\\r\\n8. **Execution advice.** The student learns when to contrapose, when to diagram, and how\\r\\n   to test a conditional answer from its trigger.\\r\\n9. **Repeatable process.** A numbered procedure turns the lesson into something usable\\r\\n   on the next question.\\r\\n10. **Practice.** Checkboxes state the work actually assigned.\\r\\n11. **Private continuation.** Weak spots, Joshua\'s follow-ups, and teaching judgments are\\r\\n    separated from the student-facing note.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What to copy and what to vary\\r\\n\\r\\nCopy the depth, hierarchy, and independence from remembered question context. Vary the\\r\\nsections to fit the lesson. Do not force every recap into the same headings. A causation\\r\\nsession, an RC mapping session, and a conditional-logic session should not look identical.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe note is complete when the student can return several days later and use it to solve\\r\\nthe same kind of problem without reconstructing the original session.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"i":1,"status":"fulfilled","output":"---\\r\\ntitle: Teaching Voice\\r\\ntype: concept\\r\\ntags: [lsat-u, voice, pedagogy, content]\\r\\ncreated: 2026-06-10\\r\\nupdated: 2026-07-18\\r\\nstatus: active\\r\\nsubject: Teaching Voice\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Teaching Voice\\r\\n\\r\\nHow LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.\\r\\n\\r\\nIt replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools \\"preserve verbatim\\" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Core voice (every register)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.\\r\\n- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student\'s own history. Praise is a specific observation, not \\"great job.\\"\\r\\n- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard (\\"expect this to be exhausting and very challenging\\"). Never be blunt about a student\'s shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.\\r\\n- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: \\"partner with you on your LSAT journey,\\" \\"getting set up,\\" \\"foundational.\\" The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.\\r\\n- **Balanced rhythm.** Short declaratives plus one slightly longer warm beat. Air between ideas. Each idea gets its own line. Real messages are short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters).\\r\\n- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype (\\"unlock,\\" \\"crush,\\" \\"level up,\\" \\"game-changer\\" — \\"crush\\" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers (\\"Sorry for such a long delay!\\"). He is not exclamation-averse.\\r\\n- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what\'s ahead.\\r\\n- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Openings, closings, and signature phrases\\r\\n\\r\\nThe mechanical fingerprint of his email, from the real corpus. Match these when drafting as Joshua.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Greeting.** \\"Hi [Name],\\" is the default (used ~519x). \\"Hey [Name],\\" for established/returning students (~229x, warmer). \\"Hello [Name],\\" (~105x) and \\"Dear [Name],\\" (~18x, formal) for first contact or a more formal note.\\r\\n- **Sign-off.** \\"Best,\\" and \\"Best regards,\\" are the workhorses (~360x / ~237x). \\"Thanks,\\" when it\'s a favor or logistics (~146x). \\"Warmly,\\" / \\"Warm regards,\\" (~54x) for a genuinely warm note. \\"Talk soon,\\" (~9x) for someone he\'ll see again soon.\\r\\n- **Signature.** Almost always signed **\\"Joshua\\"** (844 of 944 messages). The name is near-universal; include it.\\r\\n- **Signature phrases (his verbal fingerprint, use naturally, don\'t overuse):** \\"let me know\\" (by far the most common, ~390x), \\"looking forward,\\" \\"I hope\\" / \\"hope all is well\\" / \\"hope you,\\" \\"no worries at all,\\" \\"happy to\\" / \\"more than happy to,\\" \\"just wanted to make sure,\\" \\"make sure,\\" \\"no rush,\\" \\"of course.\\"\\r\\n- **Punctuation he actually uses:** semicolons (correctly, ~41x); ellipses essentially never. (For em-dashes, the hard rule in Core voice governs — never.)\\r\\n\\r\\n## The apology / accountability move\\r\\n\\r\\nOne of his most frequent and characteristic behaviors, and worth naming on its own. When he is late to reply, or missed or mishandled something, he opens by owning it plainly, often with a brief honest reason, then pivots straight to the fix. No over-apologizing, no excuses dressed up.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I\'m so sorry. I thought I had let you know ... I clearly didn\'t communicate that as well as I should have.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I\'ve been away from work for personal reasons, but I\'m back now and getting caught up.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe pattern: own it in the first line, give the real reason in one clause, move to what happens next. Warm, candid, brief.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When something on his side broke (approved shape, 2026-09-11)\\r\\n\\r\\nFrom the Sandra booking-error reply, edited by Joshua line by line over three drafts and then sent as-is. Use this shape whenever a student hit a bug or a mistake that was his. The sent text is the reference:\\r\\n\\r\\n> Hi Sandra,\\r\\n>\\r\\n> I\'m sorry about this. The error was on my end, not yours.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> To confirm: none of your bookings went through, so nothing is on the calendar yet. The page was showing a few times that the system then refused, and it looks like you landed on those. That is fixed now.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Your link is the same one as before:\\r\\n> [link]\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Pick any time it shows and it will book. If it\'s easier, just send me the time you were trying for and I\'ll add it to the schedule myself.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> If you run into any other issues, let me know.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Once again, my apologies.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Best,\\r\\n> Joshua\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Apology first, as the opening line.** \\"I\'m sorry about this. The error was on my end, not yours.\\" The ownership sits in the first two sentences, before any explanation.\\r\\n2. **State their position plainly, as a confirmation.** \\"To confirm: none of your bookings went through, so nothing is on the calendar yet.\\" Answer the question they actually asked. Don\'t mention the package or credits unless the situation calls for it.\\r\\n3. **One-sentence explanation, then \\"That is fixed now.\\"** No mechanics, no system names.\\r\\n4. **Hand them the next step, plus the manual fallback.** The link, \\"pick any time it shows and it will book,\\" and the offer to do it by hand: \\"If it\'s easier, just send me the time you were trying for and I\'ll add it to the schedule myself.\\"\\r\\n5. **Invite the next report:** \\"If you run into any other issues, let me know.\\"\\r\\n6. **Close with \\"Once again, my apologies.\\"** then \\"Best,\\" and \\"Joshua\\".\\r\\n\\r\\nCut from the draft, do not reuse:\\r\\n- \\"Sorry for the runaround\\" as a sign-off. The apology opens the email; it is not a closing quip.\\r\\n- \\"You will get a calendar invite with the Google Meet link right after.\\" Do not narrate what the system will send; it reads as a promise about machinery.\\r\\n- \\"You have all 10 sessions\\" / \\"nothing was used from your package.\\" Leave the package out unless it is needed; the reassurance they want is that nothing is booked, not an accounting.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Situational micro-templates\\r\\n\\r\\nVerbatim-grounded reference lines for the situations that recur most. Adapt, never paste blindly.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Student reschedules / cancels:** low-pressure, no guilt.\\r\\n  > No worries at all. It happens, so please don\'t worry about it. If you would like to reschedule, just let me know.\\r\\n- **He missed / no-showed (approved make-good template):** full ownership, real reason, offer to make it right (see the apology move above). When the cause was an unexpected health issue, the approved shape is: sincerely apologize, state that a serious health scare unexpectedly took him out of commission, offer a **completely free, full LSAT session**, and hand them the complimentary booking link `https://cal.com/joshua-jtx5ud/complimentary-session`. Emphasis stays limited: bold the complimentary offer, italicize the line that one focused session can put points on the board. Draft in Gmail; never send without Joshua\'s explicit go-ahead.\\r\\n- **Consultation reminder (day-of):**\\r\\n  > Just checking in before our consultation today. Looking forward to speaking with you. I just wanted to make sure everything is still good.\\r\\n- **Consultation follow-up:** warm, specific to their situation.\\r\\n  > Really nice meeting you. I enjoyed our conversation ... I\'m glad we got to talk through where you\'re at and what you\'re aiming for on test day.\\r\\n- **Inbound prospect (hasn\'t booked):** state availability plainly, no overselling, hand them the next step.\\r\\n  > Thanks for reaching out ... I\'d be happy to talk more about what you\'re working on and see if we\'d be a good fit. You can schedule a consultation here.\\r\\n- **Student under real-life stress:** meet the person first.\\r\\n  > Please don\'t apologize ... it sounds like you are dealing with an incredibly stressful and demanding situation.\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"i":2,"status":"fulfilled","output":"# Copy corrections\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery student-facing wording fix, with the reason it was wrong. Read this before\\r\\nwriting or editing copy for LSAT U so the same mistake is not made twice.\\r\\n\\r\\nCompanion to [[teaching-voice]], which says how the writing should sound. This\\r\\nsays what has actually gone wrong and why.\\r\\n\\r\\nStarted 2026-09-07 from a Read, Recall, Colour pass. Add to it every time a\\r\\ncorrection is made.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The rules these corrections came from\\r\\n\\r\\nThree rules already existed and all three leaked anyway, because each was\\r\\nenforced on the components that happened to exist when it was written. A surface\\r\\nadded later starts uncovered.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **No em dashes, ever.** [[teaching-voice]], Core voice.\\r\\n- **No internal vocabulary in student copy.** `projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md`\\r\\n  carries the glossary and marks what to avoid.\\r\\n- **Every CSS token a stylesheet uses must be defined in it.** `projects/lsatu/CLAUDE.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\nGuards now live with the content rather than with each component:\\r\\n`content/instructions-voice.test.ts` checks the authored instructions, so every\\r\\nsurface that renders them is covered, including surfaces not written yet.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Corrections\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Recall Rephrase Colour\\" → \\"Read, Recall, Colour\\"\\r\\nThe door\'s heading was hardcoded to a name the product does not have, one line\\r\\nunder a chip reading \\"Read, Recall, Colour\\". \\"Rephrase\\" belongs to a different\\r\\nActivity, Conditional Rephrase. A test asserted the wrong name, which is why\\r\\n2,600 passing tests never caught it.\\r\\n**Lesson:** when a test pins a string, check the string is right, not just that\\r\\nit matches.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Intake only. Nothing comes back out yet…\\" → plain instruction\\r\\nClinical and abstract, and the sentence then told her to read \\"with full\\r\\nintonation\\" while step one is silent reading. The clause that made intonation\\r\\ninternal, \\"hear it naturally in your head\\", had been dropped in an earlier edit.\\r\\n**Lesson:** a word like \\"intake\\" reads as jargon. And when copy is trimmed,\\r\\ncheck what the trimmed clause was load-bearing for.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Three beats through the stimulus\\" → \\"Three steps\\"\\r\\n\\"Beat\\" is the internal code name for one of the four method steps. The glossary\\r\\nsays students see **step**. The interface ten pixels below labelled the same four\\r\\nthings Step 1 to Step 4, so the page used two names for one thing.\\r\\n**Lesson:** never let an implementation name reach the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"22 pieces to read\\" → line removed\\r\\nA **piece** is one chunk, roughly a sentence, and the instructions on that same\\r\\npage use the word that way. The count was of stimuli, so \\"22 pieces\\" read as 22\\r\\nsentences.\\r\\n**Lesson:** a unit word must mean one thing per surface. Check what a word\\r\\nalready means on the page before reusing it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"say it back\\" → \\"tell it back\\", \\"The Read\\" → \\"The Recall\\"\\r\\nThe library door still carried both retired names. \\"The Read\\" was step four\'s\\r\\nformer name and collided with step one, which is why it was renamed.\\r\\n**Lesson:** a rename is not finished until every row carries it. The door was\\r\\nstale for months because it is a different database row from the sections.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"then a fourth\\" → \\"then a fourth step\\"\\r\\nThe noun sat three words back and the reader had to carry it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"once you have got all of it\\" → \\"once you have read all of it\\"\\r\\nAmbiguous between having read it and having remembered it, in the one clause\\r\\nthat triggers step four.\\r\\n**Lesson:** precision matters most where the sentence is an instruction.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The three-fact notice → one fact\\r\\n\\"Nothing is recording yet, the microphone opens on Start, and the section\\r\\nrecords in one take that you cannot pause.\\" Two of those are said again in step\\r\\nfour and in the long version, so she skipped the paragraph.\\r\\n**Lesson:** say it once, in the place it matters.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The method, told twice → the long version collapsed\\r\\nThe page rendered four numbered steps and then the whole written briefing saying\\r\\nthe same thing at length, both open. She read the method twice and could not\\r\\ntell which one to follow.\\r\\n**Lesson:** two explanations of one thing is worse than either alone.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"PT105.S1 · 24 stimuli · about 25 min\\" → removed\\r\\nThe section was already the second line of the title. The clock is at the foot\\r\\nand in the long version. The stimulus count measured the work in a unit she has\\r\\nno feel for.\\r\\n**Lesson:** delete a line that repeats what is above it. Do not reword it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Booking emails: hand over the link, not the pass settings (2026-09-08)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** \\"70 minutes each, and no expiration date, so there\'s no rush to use them.\\"\\r\\n**Keep:** \\"You\'re set up on LSAT U with 10 sessions.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nSession length and expiry are admin fields. The student sees them on the booking\\r\\npage when she picks a time, so putting them in the email is detail she did not ask\\r\\nfor and has to read past to reach the link. The credit count is worth saying because\\r\\nit tells her how much she has. Everything else about the pass stays in the backend.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** in a booking email, say who it\'s for, how many sessions, and the link.\\r\\nStop there.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"i":3,"status":"fulfilled","output":""}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '---\r\ntitle: Student name aliases\r\ntype: operating-layer\r\ntags: [students, operating-layer]\r\ncreated: 2026-07-20\r\nupdated: 2026-09-08\r\nstatus: active\r\nsubject: Student name aliases\r\naliases: []\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Student name aliases\r\n\r\nMaps the names that show up in transcript filenames, calendar invites, and email\r\nto the canonical student folder slug in `students/`.\r\n\r\nIt exists because Gemini names its exports from the meeting title, which is\r\nwhatever was typed at booking time: `Sofia`, `sofia`, `Sofia Minassian`,\r\n`LSAT Session between Joshua and Meghan Mullins` all mean one student. text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/students/sofia/recaps/2026-09-25-recap.md\n+---\n+title: Recap · Sofia · 2026-09-25\n+type: recap\n+record_kind: primary\n+student: sofia\n+session_date: 2026-09-25\n+source: transcript-downloads/2026-09-25-sofia-notes-by-gemini.md\n+status: draft\n+posted: false\n+posted_at:\n+---\n+\n+# Must Be True and Sufficient Assumption: Which Way Does the Proof Run?\n+\n+The two question types can use the same logical chain. What changes is the missing piece. In Must Be True, the statements are already given and must prove the answer. In Sufficient Assumption, the conclusion is already stated and the answer must supply enough support to prove it.\n+\n+| Question | What you have | What the answer must do |\n+|---|---|---|\n+| Must Be True | Given facts or rules | State something the given information guarantees. |\n+| Sufficient Assumption | An argument with a conclusion | Add a premise that makes the conclusion follow. |\n+\n+The wording of a question stem can be unfamiliar. Ask where the proof must start, then use that direction to identify the task.\n+\n+## For Must Be True, carry the chain forward\n+\n+Suppose a passage says that when a crop fails, nearby suppliers lose business. It also says that a fuel seller cannot sell enough to make a profit. The supported takeaway is modest: the crop failure can affect businesses beyond the farm.\n+\n+Two tempting changes would overstate the evidence:\n+\n+- **Changing the trigger:** Below-average rainfall is not the same as a failed crop. The crop could survive a slightly dry season. If the passage starts its chain at crop failure, an answer cannot start it at any rainfall below average.\n+- **Changing the result:** Losing business does not necessarily mean losing money overall. A supplier could earn less than expected and still make a profit.\n+\n+Also check whether an answer reverses the chain. From *crop failure leads suppliers to lose business*, you cannot conclude that every supplier who loses business experienced a crop failure. Suppliers can lose business for other reasons.\n+\n+Use this sequence:\n+\n+1. Mark the exact event that starts the chain.\n+2. Follow only the consequences the passage supplies.\n+3. State a conservative result before reading the choices.\n+4. For each choice, ask: Which sentence proves every part of this claim? If one part introduces a new judgment, stronger trigger, or larger consequence, set it aside.\n+\n+That last check catches words such as *responsible* or *national crisis* when the passage only describes local economic effects. A believable claim is still insufficient if the passage does not prove it.\n+\n+## For Sufficient Assumption, bridge the premise to the conclusion\n+\n+This time the conclusion is the target. Separate it from the premises, then locate the change in subject or scope.\n+\n+In the animal example, the evidence concerned living animals with similar skeletons but different hunting behavior. The conclusion concerned what we can infer about extinct animals from skeletal anatomy alone. The observation about living animals does not automatically establish the conclusion about extinct animals. The answer must connect those two ideas.\n+\n+An effective prephrase names both sides of that bridge: **if the observation about living animals holds, then skeletal anatomy alone is inadequate for the proposed inference about extinct animals.** The exact wording can vary, but the premise must reach the conclusion without an extra unstated step.\n+\n+Check a proposed assumption in two passes:\n+\n+1. Does its **if** side connect to what the premises actually establish?\n+2. Does its **then** side reach the conclusion as written, including its subject and strength?\n+\n+If either side changes the claim, the bridge is incomplete. If both connect, you have a proof. That is a better reason to keep an answer than a vague sense that its words match.\n+\n+## Use your first supported answer, then move\n+\n+On a hard question, you identified the premise-to-conclusion bridge and selected the answer that supplied it. Under a clock, keep that choice once you can explain both connections. Reopening it without a specific flaw can turn a sound inference into second guessing.\n+\n+## Before next session\n+\n+- [ ] Complete the Sufficient Assumption boot camp once it appears in your assigned work. Practice naming the premise, conclusion, and missing bridge before checking the choices.\n+- [ ] Work through the Must Be True drills you planned to do, using the exact-trigger and exact-result checks above.\n+- [ ] Take the practice test you planned for Sunday after the targeted work.\n+\n+---\n+\n+## Notes for me (not posted)\n+\n+- **Weak spots:** Sofia sometimes treats familiar stem wording as a question-type cue; she also accepted a weaker trigger and added an unsupported responsibility judgment before checking the exact claim. Sources: 00:06:36–00:07:31 and 00:17:02–00:20:46.\n+- **Improvement:** She articulated the proof direction and independently identified the Sufficient Assumption bridge on a difficult item (00:35:26 and 00:48:44–00:50:23).\n+- **Follow-ups on my side:** Verify that the Sufficient Assumption boot camp was assigned and that the promised booking link for the missed Monday meeting was added. The extra Must Be True drills were mentioned as a possibility, not a firm assignment (00:51:01–00:53:37).\n*** End Patch"))
